If I don’t sleep much, I don’t feel rested—I feel tired like anyone else would.\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing more activities than usual, like taking on extra tasks or projects, or have you noticed yourself moving around more, like pacing or fidgeting?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I feel like I’m always running hot, like I can’t shut the motor off. I’m always doing something or looking for the next thing to do, and lately that’s been causing problems for me at the firehouse.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder to focus or concentrate lately, like your attention is easily pulled away by things around you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it’s been hard. My attention gets pulled off really easily. Like a radio chirps, or somebody walks past the bay, or I just think about a call from yesterday, and I’m off what I was doing. Then it takes me a long time to get back into it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods lasting several days or longer where you felt down, hopeless, or lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I have bad days like anybody, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had several days where I felt hopeless or stopped caring about things I normally like.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your speech feels pressured lately, like you can't slow it down even if you wanted to?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I talk fast sometimes, especially when I’m worked up, but I haven’t noticed it feeling like I can’t slow it down.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any nights recently where you slept much less than usual, but still felt like you had plenty of energy the next day?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. If I sleep less, I usually feel it the next day. I haven’t had those nights where I’m running on barely any sleep and still full of energy.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

I can get intense when I’m explaining something, but I haven’t noticed it being different lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you need less sleep lately, like you feel rested even if you haven’t slept much?\n\nPatient: No, I haven’t really noticed that. If I don’t sleep much, I don’t feel rested—I feel tired like anyone else would.\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing more activities than usual, like taking on extra tasks or projects, or have you noticed yourself moving around more, like pacing or fidgeting?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I feel like I’m always running hot, like I can’t shut the motor off. My attention gets pulled off really easily. Like a radio chirps, or somebody walks past the bay, or I just think about a call from yesterday, and I’m off what I was doing. Then it takes me a long time to get back into it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods lasting several days or longer where you felt down, hopeless, or lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I have bad days like anybody, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had several days where I felt hopeless or stopped caring about things I normally like.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your speech feels pressured lately, like you can't slow it down even if you wanted to?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I talk fast sometimes, especially when I’m worked up, but I haven’t noticed it feeling like I can’t slow it down.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any nights recently where you slept much less than usual, but still felt like you had plenty of energy the next day?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. If I sleep less, I usually feel it the next day. I haven’t had those nights where I’m running on barely any sleep and still full of energy.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you need significantly less sleep than usual, like feeling fully rested after only a few hours of sleep?\n\nPatient: No, I haven’t noticed that. If I only get a few hours, I’m usually tired. I don’t feel fully rested on that little sleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble winding down or relaxing, even when there’s nothing urgent going on?\n\nPatient: Yeah.
